My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00187/index.shtml -- 187/M]] What are the conclusions of the Austrian Constitution for Family Tax Reform?
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2521	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2536	Mrs Moser, ladies and gentlemen! Of course, in recent weeks the situation has changed significantly in that the two coalition partners have reached a political agreement on family tax reform in 1998, but the starting point of these considerations was, among other things, the recognition of the constitutional judges who have essentially found that at least half of what Austrian families have to spend on their children is tax-free. The considerations in the coalition group with finance minister Edlinger, women's minister Prammer, family spokesperson Dr. Mertel and on the side of the ÖVP with our Minister of Defence Fassleven and Mrs Rauch-Kallat, went to find a solution. We have found it, and I think it has become a very tangible solution because it does not bring Austria's families less than 12.6 billion schilling per year, per child and month 500 S, per child and year 6 000 S more in family allowance and child drop-off in one. I believe that this increase in financial transfers by almost 30 per cent, i.e. almost a third, is something which, as a family package of its own, is really a decisive milestone in this country's family policy.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2566	Additional question? Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2581	Federal Minister! What further priorities will be In addition to these monetary benefits, do you sit?

ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e2614	As important as the financial transfers for our families are, of course, family policy is only a small part of my view associated with financial issues. It's also about different things. Some of these we will have to discuss this afternoon in a request: I mean the issue of violence in the family, the sexual abuse of children, which unfortunately God also occurs in families. I don't want to deny these shadows at all. It is part of a family policy to also deal with accepting these things. However, I should like to point out clearly that a very important focus in my view this year – and we are also working hard together with the coalition partner – is the better compatibility of family and profession. This is, in fact, the big topic for many young women: the compatibility of family and work, that is to have children, but at the same time not to lose or recover the workplace. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] This is a subject where the legislator can only do something on condition. This is mainly about raising awareness, as it is about showing Austrian employers that they need young, committed, motivated women, that it is good to invest in farm kindergartens, that it is good to offer young mothers, but also fathers flexible working hours, because it is always clear that the decisive person is as free as possible over his own working time. It is not so much a question of wanting to work only 20, 18 or 25 hours a week, but of being free to arrange working time as far as possible. To open the way there is certainly an important task for us, and if we have done so, then I think we are a bit further towards a better reconciliation of family and profession.

I would like to point this out in a specific example: Unfortunately, in Austria, God’s marriages are increasingly being divorced, and there is also the legal opinion that although it is good that the divorced woman, who is normally entitled to care, continues to receive the family allowance and the amount of child replacement, there can be no doubt at all that it will continue to be so – but today these payments are not acceptable to the husband’s maintenance obligations. We, the coalition partners, Finance Minister Edlinger, Women's Minister Prammer and I, have therefore clearly defined ourselves: From our political point of view, divorce should be private, it is not a matter of the legislator, it is not a matter of the government to give divorced couples more financial resources than those living in upright marriage. But this is certainly a thought approach and a possible basis for clarifying through a constitutional provision: This is the political will of the legislator. We want positive discrimination on the part of the married family in order to give the constitutional judges, as the President of the Constitutional Court, Adamovich, pointed out, some assistance in order to be able to judge this question in the future as I have just described it.

In this responsibility we have found this family tax reform. It is not easy to realize 12.6 billion schilling, but I believe that through a very partnership between Minister of Finance Edlinger and me and by a very reasonable distribution of the burdens, on the one hand, on the budget, on the other, on the funds from the family compensation fund, this family pot, which is thankful to God in Austria, we have come to a solution that is responsible. The financing is ensured, and I must stress that it is, of course, partly a prelude to the major tax reform of 2000. But now, as a family minister, I say: Nothing should be more right for me than for families to be considered in the context of a major tax reform and to play a role in this respect.

I also reject allegations that the Federal officials are automatically more inefficient than the national servants. I'm sure you can't say that. So: What is this state reform about? – The question of this state reform is that the service is provided where it can best be provided for the citizen. This means that we do not want this state reform to be based on ideological principles – we want to weaken the communities, we want to weaken the federal government and only strengthen the countries or vice versa, no matter what – but on the principle of civil kindness. It is precisely for this reason that I have also proposed that, in addition to this technical question of the transfer of the indirect federal administration to the countries, we consider a new reform of the principles of our state that we are clearly and clearly and without ideological doubts rethinking the division of tasks between municipalities, countries and the federal government in our system, in accordance with the principle where it is best for the citizen and where the tasks can most efficiently be done. This is the second step in a major reform of the division of tasks of our state.

Then: At present, on average, we wait four to eight weeks for an answer from the Ministries. In some cases, there are outsiders where we have to wait up to five, six, seven months and even up to several years. However, if we were to set down the twelve weeks by law, it would probably be true that every ministry would use the twelve weeks from the outset, and this would significantly extend the period of reply to the state of the state. Please bear in mind that there is an increasing emphasis on mediation by the judicial system in Austria. On all possible occasions, the Minister of Justice repeatedly points out that he wants to make substantial savings possible through mediation in the future – savings in favour of the administration and savings in favour of the citizen. Parliament now decides that an administrative area will be excluded from an existing and functioning mediation and that the citizen will be referred to civil law. So this is exactly the reverse development: The courts go toward mediation, and the administration refers people to the civil law path. I believe that this is not a good way for the Austrian citizen. – Thank you for your attention.

Point Report of the Constitutional Committee on the Government Template (889 of the Supplements): Framework Convention on the Protection of National Minorities and Declaration (1067 of Supplements)
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e7091	We'll get to the 2nd. Agenda item. Since there has been no oral reporting, we can start the debate immediately. I shall be the first speaker to give Mr Mag. Try the word. Voluntary time limit: 10 minutes.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e7118	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! The Framework Convention on the Protection of National Minorities is the first multilateral, legally binding instrument dedicated to the specific protection of minorities. It has now been ratified by the 20 states and is now on the 1st floor. February 1998 – entered into force. In order for it to be accepted by the 40 members of the Council of Europe as a treaty, the term "minority" has not been defined. This is certainly a weakness of the framework agreement. The signatory States may define the definition themselves. Nevertheless, it is an extremely important legal instrument of the Council of Europe, particularly with regard to the situation of minorities in Eastern Europe. The present Framework Convention defines the legal principles to which States commit themselves to the protection of national minorities. A number of pragmatic provisions and objectives are specified. However, a large margin of discretion is granted in the achievement of these objectives by the individual States. So, as I said, there is no definition, but one has chosen a pragmatic procedure. This means that the Convention will be implemented by means of national legislation and relevant government policies that may follow. For Austria, this means, first, that there is agreement that the Convention shall apply to those referred to in Article 1 (1). 2 groups of persons defined by the People's Groups Act are to apply. The Parties undertake to fight against discrimination, to promote the equality of the ethnic groups, to preserve their culture, to guarantee freedom of assembly, association, thought, conscience, religious and opinion, and above all to allow the use of the minority language as the most important criterion for their identity and to allow the establishment of their own schools and training facilities and so on. The Convention also contains its own control mechanism. The reporting obligation of the Contracting States – which is particularly important – is also ensured. The Contracting States shall, in accordance with the Convention, provide for objectives and practices aimed at the assimilation of members of a minority, as well as in analogy to Annex III to the Vienna Declaration, take appropriate measures to combat racism, xenophobia, anti-Semitism and intolerance to protect people from discriminatory, hostile or violent acts. Austria has certainly largely fulfilled its contractual obligations. Nevertheless, a number of wishes and hopes remain open – and they have not yet been fulfilled. This is why, as in the past, I would like to reiterate and reaffirm some of these hopes and wishes: on the one hand, to decide on a constitutional objective in which Austria acknowledges its minorities and its linguistic, cultural and ethnic diversity. Although such a state objective would not be particularly effective, it would be a clear commitment by Austria to its peoples. I would also like to stress the need to set up a general-Austrian conference on the co-ordination of national objectives and ideas, as was already done in a very fruitful manner in the previous year. This would include an institutionalised representative body that could enter into negotiations with the Federal Government or with the national governments. This would be a very, very important step towards a consistent development of an emancipatory government policy for the benefit of the peoples, and it would help to summarize and represent the different positions of the ethnic groups in Austria – whether it be the Croatians, or the Slovenians. I am also gurgising – this is especially your address, Mr State Secretary! – the ratification of the European Charter of Regional and Minority Languages, which Austria signed in 1992, the second important international Convention of the Council of Europe on the Protection of Minorities. Perhaps it is possible to present this document to Parliament for ratification this year. With these two important international legal instruments for the protection of minorities, I believe that the perspective shifts from the narrow national level to the international, European level. It corresponds to a good European spirit and to the integrative forces, and it is directed against national chauvinism. That is why I welcome the ratification of this agreement, the framework convention, as a first step which may be followed by good national steps.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e7175	The next speaker is Mr Dr. Mock. 10 minutes of voluntary time limit. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e7196	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! If we today – the preliminary consultations allow us to accept this – a significant treaty, namely the Framework Convention on the Protection of National Minorities, this is a politically important process, for the following reasons: For the first time, a protection agreement for minorities under legal liability is put under legal obligation. It has legal effect, and thus for the first time essential fundamental and freedom rights to members of an ethnic group are bound by legal obligations. It is actually surprising that there has been no such thing. For the first time – this is a remarkable event. Secondly, This safeguarding of fundamental and freedom-related rights gives an opportunity to make progress in democracy policy. For the majority then behaves as we do in the Constitution: Before the law, every citizen is the same – a matter of which, however, members of minorities or even citizens do not always believe that it corresponds to reality. Thirdly, strengthening minority rights means motivating the full social commitment of the members of minorities. You now know that they are at home in that state. In this home they feel comfortable and are welcome in all areas. Finally – and this is not the last argument for this – this Convention strengthens the internal stability of state units and external security with the content of strengthening minority rights. In Austria – with its special situation and from history – we are in a situation where minorities on the one hand correspond to the border of the majority on the other, and this is a good understanding and good neighbourly relationship due to the solid anchoring of minority rights. The situation of relations between Austria and Slovenia or between Austria and Hungary is also crucial for our security. We are not alone in being able to stand for our security. Good, stable relations with neighbouring countries are an important factor in our foreign policy and an important factor in external security. This was the case when we tried to build bridges with the neighbourhood policy across the Iron Curtain, and this commitment to the Neighbourhood Policy is also important now, at the time of the turn, at which there are democratic regimes there, but which are faced with a terrible heritage from the time of Communism. This is our interest and our task. We also have a bridge function in the coming decades. The 1993 Council of Europe Summit was a success, because within nine months this Convention was drawn up, which says to minorities: You have a greater chance of more equality, more equality! ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e7306	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The good thing about the present proposal is that it is the first legally binding multinational attempt to implement an agreement in favour of national minorities. The content is shabby, and that's the negative. My previous speakers have also made it sound like this: There is actually little substance in it, and what is in it is of low weight. Of particular importance in the negative sense, I believe that, while the formal equality of the members of national minorities with the members of the respective majority population is emphasized, it is explicitly stated that no collective rights can be derived from it for a national minority or a group of people, I prefer to use the expression "people group" because it appears less diminutive than the term "minority". We all know, however, that a people's group can only be preserved in the long term, that it can only flourish and thrive if it is recognized as a group and if it is granted rights and opportunities as a group. In the stage-by-step order of negation of a positive group policy, there is the position that France usually takes, but also that of Bulgaria and Turkey, which say: There are no minorities in us! All Turks in Bulgaria are only mountain Bulgarians, and all Kurds in Turkey are only mountain towers. In France, there are no minorities at all at all, although we know that there are a handful of minorities there. But the second stage is that, although the individual citizen of a minority is formally granted the same rights as the citizen of the majority population, it does not recognise group rights. And about this modest stage: Every citizen is equal!, regrettably, the present proposal does not go out. It is also not a question of pretending, for example, that members of the national minorities, of the ethnic groups would be able to provide training, education, teaching in the mother tongue – kindergarten, school, everything there is – it is only that this is allowed. That means: If a person wants to set up and operate a kindergarten privately in the language of the minority, then it cannot be prevented by any means, or at least one should not do so. But there is no point in saying that there is a claim to what is actually natural to get lessons in the mother tongue in Austria. When it comes to language problems, the formulations are depressing and defying at the same time. It means, for example, that you already have to break on the tongue: Any person belonging to a national minority has the right to use their minority language privately and freely and freely in the public. That is to say, he can speak in his language, at home and even on the street, but when it comes to the official language, it is only the point that efforts should be made to get to the administrative authorities to make themselves understood in the minority language, in the ethnic group language. The courts are not even referred to in this tame formulation. Only the administrative authorities are involved in the intention to make an effort, but the courts are not even mentioned in this modest intention, although this seemed particularly important to me. This means that it is a first attempt, it is obviously the smallest common denominator that can be found in Europe. It is formal in itself a certain progress, material but none at all. It will be necessary to make an effort to infuse this form of life. We can only hope that it will succeed, first of all, the bit that is inside, the bit that it means to really enforce, so that it does not remain empty paper. I understand a number of countries in which it will be very difficult to live up to these modest approaches. Secondly, we will have to try to ensure that we really have a certain level, as has been practiced in Austria for a long time – we are all proud of it, and what happens to us is still not enough – even in the other countries – here I look only from the northeast to the east to the south-east of Austria – in this modest framework. That must be our concern. Let us be glad that there is the framework, but the framework alone is not enough. A meaningful picture will have to come in.

Nevertheless, I am very pleased that this framework agreement is being ratified today, because I see it as an important and necessary instrument of Europe's political reorganisation. It shows that Europe is It is true that there has been a change in attitude and in thinking about national minorities and groups of people. While in earlier times minorities have been regarded by the national states as being more at risk of their territorial integrity, so to speak as potential irrationists, it is recognized today that groups represent an enrichment, a bridge to the neighbours, a bridge to Europe at all. It is therefore important that such an instrument exists, even if its content could be better, as the Parliamentary Assembly has also demanded, that there be an additional protocol to the European Convention on Human Rights. I still believe that such an additional protocol would be correct and good. Today's protection of national minorities is undoubtedly also an important instrument for preventing conflict and maintaining peace in Europe. I believe that every observer in the history of time is clear that the conflict in the former Yugoslavia began to bring about the abolition of autonomy in Vojvodina and Kosovo when national minorities were granted their rights. At this moment other peoples of Yugoslavia have rightly shown distrust and fear of the regime in Belgrade, so this instrument is good and right to avoid conflict. The protection of minorities and the rights and freedoms of the members of minorities is expressed in this framework convention as an integral part of human rights. Thus, minority rights are no longer an internal matter of a state itself, but rather a matter of the European international community, that is, an international commitment. We are also committed to this international commitment today by this ratification. I have some experience as rapporteur of the Parliamentary Assembly for countries with minorities, for example in Slovakia, Romania and Turkey – both positive and negative. The Council of Europe has laid down very specific minority rights in the Parliamentary Assembly, in resolution 1201 – even on the question of visiting school and the minority language before the authorities – and collective rights. And we have brought about this political resolution, which does not have an immediate legal effect, by the fact that the Council of Europe, that is, the Parliamentary Assembly, insisted that only those states which have been known to do so have been included in the Council of Europe, both from their governments and from their parliaments. That was the case with Romania, that was the case with Bulgaria, that was the case in other countries, and that means that de facto, too, a quite enforceable claim arose and could be enforced from an initially not completely clear legal situation. If today, for example, there is a complete change in the attitude towards minorities in Romania or Bulgaria, for example, towards the Turks in Bulgaria and Hungary in Romania, as the largest minorities to which there have always been tensions, this should be due to these efforts and this agreement in the Council of Europe and the commitment to respect what has been promised there, because the Council of Europe has at the same time decided in the Parliamentary Assembly to review this too. This is the result of the European Union's determination that one of the points to be met for accession to the European Union is the recognition and implementation of human rights and, secondly, the recognition of minorities. That's what's inside, too. That is why I would like to say to my colleague, Mr President: This, of course, also results in the need to eliminate provisions that are illegal to human rights, for example the Beneš decree. I do not mean the abolition of all Beneš decrees, but those which are clearly illegal to human rights. [[Congratulation with the ÖVP and the Abg. Arbitrator.]] I think this is a remarkable progress that could be made with it. One last: Austria is therefore also recognized. And we must not be ashamed of our group policy, Mrs Stoisits, because it is really recognized and honoured by all the other countries!

Overall, the stated benefits for the Federal Ministry of the Defense of the State were not insignificant in the event of joint awarding to a bidder. As foreseen, the offers were initially subject to the cost-benefit assessment on an individual basis (by destination allocation and deep-fly radar). A further step was taken to compare the offers for both uses together and in all possible combinations. The most favourable result of this arrangement was the joint award to the French bidder. The members of the evaluation committee unanimously approved the assessment result. After approval of the opinion of the Assessment Commission by the Deputy Director of the BMLV section responsible for armor, procurement and supply, it was adopted on 25 July. In July 1994, the Federal Minister for the Defence of the State, Dr. Werner Fassleven, collected and taken from him on 31 December. ==References== The Court of Auditors gave a favourable opinion on the work of the Evaluation Commission and considered that its composition was appropriate. The cost-benefit comparison was in line with the procedure laid down in the Federal Ministry of Defence. The Court of Auditors could not determine the preference or disadvantage of bidders. In the case of the undivided surcharge for both systems, the offers included, in particular, price advantages. A comparison of the evaluations showed that the joint award finally carried out compared to a separate award had achieved a more favourable supply value of around 51 million schillings and a value of approximately 4 percent higher. This confirmed the economic efficiency and effectiveness of the process of the Federal Ministry of State Defense to combine the two procurement operations without excluding the allocation of the award for the two individual systems." High house! ==References====External links== In February 1995, the Court of Auditors issued an audit order which had been very extensive. All major procurements of the Federal Ministry of State Defense in the time of the 1. The Court of Auditors' audit will be carried out by January 1987 until 1995, taking into account in particular the compliance with the provisions in force, the efficiency of the decision criteria, the efficiency of internal and external control mechanisms, and the suspicions of illegal payments. ==References====External links== There were shared views between the government parties on the one hand and the opposition parties on the other, on the other, on the extent to which the Court of Auditors' responsibility for this audit task can be meaningful and targeted, on the other hand, on the other. The scepticism expressed by some speakers in this context went in two directions: On the one hand, it was doubted whether the Court of Auditors would be able to examine the wide range of procurement cases. On the other hand, scepticism was expressed as to whether the Court of Auditors was at all the appropriate parliamentary instrument for the detection of illegal payments. The second moment, i.e. the question of whether the Court of Auditors is capable of discovering illegal payments, was raised by the Court of Auditors. Opinion delivered on 21 February 1995. I did it literally at the time: "The Court of Auditors is not entitled to examine the request made by the National Council to see if the National Council should not have used other instruments." – End of my own quote. I am still in this position. I understand that there are different opinions in this House and that they have also been expressed. However, I also ask for understanding that it cannot be the task of the Court of Auditors to examine his mandate, which he has received, in order to determine whether other control instruments should not have been used. I have also added in this context that the Court of Auditors will do all it can to meet the confidence it has given it through the award of this mandate. As far as the objection to the Court of Auditors' audit is concerned, I must admit that even when the Court of Auditors was confronted with the audit order, it was a very long time before the Court of Auditors' advice on how best to meet this request. For it must not be overlooked that not less than 38 000 cases of procurement have occurred since 1987 until 1995 and that the selection of which is now the most essential has not been so easy. In addition, it should not be mentioned that the total purchase value has accounted for around 45 billion schilling. The Court of Auditors has, in order to be able to fulfil this enormous task, used a sampling technique which it has first developed on the basis of this specific examination, in cooperation with two university professors, namely Professor Dr. Grün and Dr. Mochty, whom I would like to express my thanks from this point of view on behalf of the Court of Auditors, especially since it – and this should not be mentioned – has carried out these activities, this support for the Court of Auditors free of charge. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] This sampling technique made it possible for the Court of Auditors to select the most important cases of procurement and to carry out a very in-depth examination, with around 8 billion schillings in the procurement value of the contracts examined by it. These were all major orders, namely the procurement of the armed helicopter, the anti-terrorist weaponry, the light anti-aircraft weaponry, the radar equipment, the armored wheeled vehicles and also the air transport systems that gave rise to the test for the order to the Court of Auditors. The Court of Auditors has made very critical reports and has already been made by some of my previous speakers. It also noted, in its view, significant shortcomings, particularly in the planning of principles, in the planning of introductions, in the execution of payments and in a partly insufficient documentation. On the other hand, the Court of Auditors also did not save praise where there was praise. It should be stressed here, for example, that the Federal Ministry of Defence's control office, which, according to the Court of Auditors, has really done and does excellent work as an internal control body. What conclusion does the Court of Auditors draw from this very extensive examination which it has carried out? – On the one hand, the National Council has been able to provide a wealth of information, which is once again a point of departure for very targeted action to be taken from its side, for action to be taken and for the purpose of dealing with individual procurement in the Committee very specifically and in detail. On the other hand, the Court of Auditors itself has gained some new insights, both as regards its audit technology and its audit methodology, and as regards the further insights into the procurement of the Federal Ministry of State Defense. As the Court of Auditors' wish, it remains that the recommendations it has made will also be implemented. This request is addressed on the one hand to the Ministry of State Defense, on the other hand also to the National Council with the request to support the Court of Auditors in the implementation of the recommendations and to do everything in the future that is appropriate for the national defence, but also to the taxpayer in connection with the procurement of the national defence. I believe that the Court of Auditors has done a great deal of work and, as I believe, has fulfilled the satisfaction of the National Council. This was expressed in the speeches of my previous speakers. If the Court of Auditors were to be thanked by all the parties, I must say that this thanks which I have received with great goodwill applies to the auditors and officials of the Court of Auditors who have really done great things. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and with representatives of the SPÖ.]] [[President Dr. Fischer shall take over the chairmanship.]] In conclusion, I believe that the Court of Auditors must have complied with the confidence given to it by the National Council in the award of this mandate.

Furthermore, since 1997, the Criminal Code has also provided for higher threats to the mere reproduction or dissemination of such products, and the mere possession or manufacture of such products is also punishable. Questions 13 and 14: Of course, the Federal Government is committed to an ongoing improvement in the legal status of victims in criminal law and in criminal proceedings. This is a permanent task which cannot be done by means of a single bill. As examples, I would just like to mention the procedural witness protection programme and the introduction of gentle hearings of children or of vulnerable victims and witnesses. As part of the next steps, the Federal Government will redesign the facts and limitation periods in the area of sexual child abuse, extend the limitation periods and extend the possibility of a gentle hearing to all victims of sexual offences. With the introduction and the ongoing development of the Law on Crime Victims, Austria has taken on a pioneering role here internationally. In place of the injured person, the Federal Government shall provide intermediate benefits for the victim's claim for damages and, if they are transferable, shall, of course, recover from the perpetrator. These compensation benefits have increased continuously in recent years and will be extended even further. The General Civil Code was also supplemented last year by the creation of a general right to compensation for the intangible damage suffered by victims of sexual offences. ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e12958	Mr President. My venerations Ladies and gentlemen! As the Federal Minister for Justice, I should like to point out, with all due clarity, that the judicial body has for years gone a consistent legal path to improving our legal order. I have already clearly disproved the march of one-eyed, perpetrator-friendly measures of my resort on the occasion of the urgent question in the Federal Council two weeks ago. Of the recent demand that, at least in certain cases, life must last from the outset – a claim which I have already decided and rejected in the past and for good reasons – at least in some cases, the questioner has long since been dismissed, as the statement on page 2 shows. I assume that this has also been done for good reasons. Ladies and gentlemen! Our sexual criminal law has already been partially renewed in 1989 in a first step. Further steps have been taken in the field of child pornography in 1994 and 1996, most recently with the marked increase in the threats of punishment already addressed by the Federal Chancellor. Recently, all the political groups in this House have been invited to present proposals in the working group called for by me, especially to re-establish the criminal relations in the area of sexual acts with children, and all parties have initially made use of them – in particular, in order to re-establish the criminal relations in the area of sexual acts with children, so that all considencies-like acts will in future be subject to the more severe threats of punishment of up to ten years – in the event of serious consequences up to 20 years – as well as to an extension of the period of limitation beyond the age of old age, for example, in the sense that limitations should occur at least three to five years after the completion of adulthood. In addition, there are already proposals to further expand the counter-dictive, gentle hearing introduced in Austria as one of the first European states in 1993, particularly of children as victims of crime, in particular for raped women. The next legislative step thus prepared will be subject to a general review in the coming weeks and will be available to the National Council before the summer. Similarly, the Federal Ministry of Justice has been trying for years to take into account the so-called "reward coverage of the victim" in criminal and criminal proceedings. An important stage was the Criminal Process Change Act 1993 with the Criminal Procedural Witness Protection Programme and the above-mentioned gentle hearing of unmistakable and other victims as witnesses. In this way, I also see the gradual expansion of the out-of-court settlement, by which the needs of the victims for satisfaction and reparation could be quickly and unbureaucratically met in 32,000 cases, although as a measure in the interests of the victims; but it does not, I would like to say quite clearly, come into question for the crimes discussed today, especially those of child abuse, because the necessary conditions – no serious debt, no special special and general concerns – are all not given. However, it seems to me to be worth mentioning that in developing a comprehensive disversion concept – thereby reducing the weight of crimes, taking particular account of the interests and claims of the victims – it is planned that the revenue from the fines to be paid by the suspects should be used substantially to support victim assistance and victim advice facilities, primarily in the area of aggression and sexual offences against children. Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, the legal status of those victims whose most personal legal assets – in particular sexual integrity – have been seriously injured or seriously threatened by crimes is to be reinforced as a result of the forthcoming re-design of the criminal procedure and in particular by the imposition of material damage. Before the implementation of this reform project, however, it seems urgent to teach victims of criminal offences – especially in the area of sexual criminal law – more comprehensive than they have so far been about the possibilities available to them at criminal proceedings, as well as at civil and social law level. A brochure written by my department is being completed. After that, victims will be better able to use existing information and design rights as well as assistance services. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Finally, with the Austrian Bar Association, the establishment of a first deliberation for crime victims was agreed at the District Courts. As an acute aid, an Austrian hotline for victims of abuse is also to be established. Through the possibility of also using anonymous competent advice, sexually abused young people should be advised to turn to people who can help them with confidence. [[Abg. May. Stadler: How is a three-year-old child supposed to do this, Minister? Three-year-olds don't report!]] No, but there are also raped women, and there are parents of raped children, so there are quite... [[Abg. May. Stadler: These are usually the perpetrators when it happens in the family!]] Of course, it is even better to use the professional official bodies. It is necessary to be able to use these even in the future without an advertisement obligation. [[Abg. May. Stadler: Because the authorities don't do anything!]] [[Abg. Dr. Haider: That's what it means to bring the youth offices to the front!]] The area of justice has remained in no way inactive in all the areas addressed today, but has responded early on to new social needs and needs, but also to expectations. In this context, highly respected innovative solutions have also been developed internationally, which have been modelled by other countries. This work on the gradual improvement of the legal system is being carried out intensively in this year. We are not waiting for spectacular occasions to propose unthinked rapid shots, ladies and gentlemen – almost through public calls! No, in this sensitive area of criminal law – as in hardly any other area of legal policy – the forward-looking and accompanying, partly multidisciplinary, technical discussion, the appropriateness and balance of legislative solutions is often a result of the consideration and concern of a multidisciplinary process, which cannot be ignored or reduced unpunished. This multidisciplinary approach is particularly important in the present context. In this sense, Mr Dr. Krüger, I have said that punishment alone is not enough, but it is first of all the brightening of the taboo zone of sexual abuse... [[Abg. Dr. Haider: No one has claimed that you should only punish!]] It was criticized, however, that I said that punishment alone is not enough. [[Abg. Dr. Haider: For this there must be an advertisement obligation!]] [[Congratulations with SPÖ and ÖVP.]] [[Abg. Dr. Haider: You are against the ad obligation! [[Abg. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: There are more abuses!]] It is – I see it – an important task for the whole society to be a network of listening and looking at. Only then do we have a chance to intervene and remedy, a chance to brighten up the dark field, which still surrounds many children in distress. If criminal investigations occur, the ad does not decide as such, but the delicate evidence of facts about success and failure of administrative intervention. All professionals and all relevant professional groups agree that in this way there is no return to awareness and encouragement, in this way of creating a network of responsibility against violence and sexual assaults, especially no return to the indifferent obligation to display, which has made it so easy to shift responsibility and does not light up the great dark field. In particular, the restriction of public authorities with a trust mark has helped to reduce barriers to the use of advice and assistance. Ladies and gentlemen! Preventing and combating sexual offences against children and adolescents requires a clear signal of social and criminal concern as well as the greatest possible consideration of the interests of victims. But even more, it needs a comprehensive climate of education, social attention and sensitivity to the needs and silent calls of our children, a climate of professional and knowledgeable handling of suspicions, as well as optimal cooperation and networking of all parties involved. We must not leave children alone in this situation, in a situation of great need for them. We must encourage and support the environment of these children – above all the mothers and the possible contact persons, including from social professions – to care. Only then will the intervention of law enforcement bodies take place at the right time and have a chance of success. The more frequent it is, the more likely it is to have a preventive effect on potential offenders. Therefore, the brightening of the dark field is not as important as the level of punishment to help those who have become victims as well as to avoid new victims. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e14352	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Sexual violence, sexual abuse – it has already been said – hit girls and women to a high degree. Sexual violence and sexual abuse are centuries old and have been expressed with many different means. I remember: until today as a means of war, just to mention one. Sexual violence and sexual abuse became precisely because women were directly and incredibly often confronted by women – slowly, much too slow, but they were women. I therefore say: If we are discussing the issue of sexual violence and sexual abuse today and want to make it public and do everything against it, then we have to thank very many women in the past who have not looked away and have not kept their mouths shut. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] On the one hand, it would almost have to satisfy us with satisfaction that so much has been done for us. I myself can still remember very well times when it was necessary to occupy houses to install women's houses, which were often referred to as holiday homes. I can still remember very well times when a lot of private people and also people in the public have looked away and negated or disputed this topic. Today, after this long journey, we have institutionalized a lot, the institutions are more and more present, the funds are being made available, the remedies are being improved, and this issue comes out of taboo. Thank God: We're suing, we're showing up and we're fighting. I think that the whole of society is very well advised to continue this course very seriously, without polemics and without abuse of this subject. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ.]] I also think we have a lot to do here. It is often and often not a shame to the perpetrators, because they are perpetrators, but shame to the victims, because they are still presented as such. We still often have the broad acceptance in society that violence in the family, sexual violence is something that one prefers to have nothing to do with and what one wants to discuss away. It is necessary that the numbers of ads, but also those of the proceedings and convictions, rise. But in order for things to change socially in general, of course we need the sense of society and, hopefully, the sense of the perpetrators to have done something with which they have hurt individuals and society as a whole. It cannot be assumed that this issue of sexual violence and sexual abuse is, in part, still presented as a cavalier offence, that many times in society, in their own families and in the neighborhood, no civil appeal is being practised, because one wants nothing to do with this unpleasant subject. We need intensive work with the children so that these children can also defend themselves so that they can get up and say, there is injustice to me, something happens to me that I do not need to accept, what I can refuse and what I can defend against. But this also requires a general discussion of sexuality, and it also needs a new quality of an open and modern society. We have set up many things, put in place many measures and implemented many individual projects, and we are happy to spend more and more money to guarantee them all. But only together will we be able to achieve that the abuse of children – a subject which we will never be able to take entirely from the agenda – will be assessed in this society, as we can be responsible to children and women, only together can we achieve that we can face these children and women in the way we want to face them. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e16511	The President of the Court of Auditors is now on the floor. Please, Mr President.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e16532	Mr President. Mr. Federal Minister! High house! The current case of examination differs from others normally perceived by the Court of Auditors by the time dimension. I should like to remind you that the Court of Auditors had already made the first attempt in spring 1992 to examine Bank Austria – in spring 1992, when my predecessor, President Dr. Broesigke, was still in charge of the Court of Auditors. In the spring of 1992, two refusals were made by Bank Austria, and the Court of Auditors was forced to refer the matter to the Constitutional Court for clarification. The Constitutional Court took a positive view of the Court of Auditors in the spring of 1993, i.e. one year after the Court of Auditors, following which the Court of Auditors made a recent, i.e. a third, examination attempt, in which, surprisingly, this distinguishes the case from other similar cases in which the Court of Auditors has also refused to examine the body in question, but it is quite crucial – the Bank of Austria has refused to enter the Court of Auditors once again, despite the present constitutional court knowledge of the Court of Auditors. The Court of Auditors therefore saw itself in a situation where, although it had a positive knowledge of the Constitutional Court, it could not be examined by the Court of Justice for lack of the possibility of exercising this knowledge. In this situation, which was not only unsatisfactory for the Court of Auditors, but which also involved the risk of a certain prejudiciality to other bodies subject to its examination, the National Council strongly supported the Court of Auditors. The National Council did not find itself with this situation of its audit body and has provided a means of ensuring that the Court of Auditors could still have its right. However, it had to amend the Federal Constitution and also the Constitutional Court Act in such a way that the findings of the Constitutional Court were granted a possibility of execution in a case of jurisdiction, so since then the findings of the Constitutional Court have included an enforceability clause. I see in this support, which the National Council gave to the Court of Auditors at the time, a sign of the cooperation and good will of the National Council towards the Court of Auditors. From this point of view, I have already done so elsewhere, I can thank the National Council for this support. [[Congratulations with SPÖ, ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Finally, the amendment of the Constitution adopted by the National Council was decisive in order that the Court of Auditors had made another, fourth attempt, which, however, was also considered with regard to a negative attitude by the Bank of Austria, but that the Court of Auditors was then able to refer it to the Constitutional Court again and subsequently gained further knowledge of the Constitutional Court in 1995. With this insight, it has finally succeeded in carrying out an examination at Bank Austria in the fifth run. It was, therefore, a scenario quite different from the usual examinations, which the Court of Auditors had to register. It was partly accompanied by spectacular media reports. The Court of Auditors itself has always assessed the situation a little more soberly. He considered the dispute or, better said, the dispute with Bank Austria to be a sign of differences of opinion as regards the Court of Auditors' competence to examine. In the end, it has also succeeded in directing this conflict as a legal conflict into an orderly legal procedure designated by the Constitution and in carrying out it there in a way similar to that of a rule of law. I would like to stress, therefore, that although I see a certain singularity in the behaviour of Bank Austria, I see in the final analysis the expression of a legal view which was different from that of the Court of Auditors and which led to it - I would like to see it very positively - that, on the one hand, the Constitution has now brought further rights to the Court of Auditors, precisely on the basis of the revision I have mentioned, and that on the other hand, I would also like to say this positively, there is also an undertaking in front of the Constitutional Court that not only the territorial authority which is to be attributed to the undertaking enjoys the position of parties but also the undertaking itself. I also consider this to be progress, and I should like to point out in this connection that this is a demand which the Court of Auditors had set up a long time ago and which could be fulfilled with this constitutional innovation. I would also like to stress that, despite this period of time, with all the accompanying appearances, the audit behaviour of the Bank Austria was very cooperative and the recruitment of the auditors of the Court of Auditors towards the Bank of Austria was in no way affected by what had happened in advance. It was – I would like to say so – the scenario of this period of time, this period before the examination, more spectacular than the Court of Auditors itself saw, because the Court of Auditors always considered it a legal difference. The Court of Auditors has therefore carried out the examination, of course, has not been influenced by this scenario, but only by the objectives of the examination which it has to observe as well as in all the other examinations. He has also, as he has done in other tests, not limited himself to reporting a defect, but has also highlighted the positives of the body under examination. These were to be found at Bank Austria, which has already been referred to by several of my previous speakers. There were, of course, some shortcomings in the Bank Austria as well as in any audited body, which the Court of Auditors presented in its report. A number of criticisms have been incorporated into the report. I have to say, too, that the behaviour and receipt of Bank Austria has been remarkable, because some shortcomings have already been eliminated in the course of the examination. If some Members, including Mr Trattner, have said that the Court of Auditors' report is no more complete, I can hardly disagree with it. I take note of that too. I would just like to stress that the Court of Auditors' audit cannot be complete at any body examined by it. This would require an overall examination which would extend over months, even years. The Court of Auditors clearly must make a selection of the sub-divisions to be examined by the body under examination, and cannot, in turn, examine other areas. This is in the nature of the matter and has been in line with the Court of Auditors' opinion and practice for many years, so that there is no deviation in what the Court of Auditors' audit is about to do. The Court of Auditors will probably be able to turn to other areas if it comes into the situation once again to examine the Bank of Austria, and will no longer be able or unable to undergo such a detailed examination of those areas which it has already carried out in the examination it has carried out. This is also due to the fact that the Court of Auditors selects sub-areas which, not least, depend on their actuality during each audit. Mr Trattner also questioned whether the 65 million interest savings mentioned in the Court of Auditors' report were not too low. I have to admit, too, that this can be the case. However, I would like to stress once again what I have already done in the Court of Auditors: The 65 million Schilling savings in interest rates, which are found in this report, are a regular result. They are linked to the day-to-day and refer to March 1996. I have already said in the Committee that this saving of interest is dependent on the course of business and that it can change. Interest savings may be larger to another deadline, but may also be lower. It can - I do not want to exclude this - reach a three-digit million, just as Mr Trattner did. Only at the time when we did the examination, on which we did a major survey, at that time was the interest savings in the order of 65 million schilling given. However, more importantly than the fact that interest savings are actually – it is twenty million – is the conclusion that should be drawn from it and which the Court of Auditors has drawn in its report; the conclusion that a liability commission should be paid for it as equivalent from the Bank Austria of the Municipality of Vienna. There is a recommendation from the Court of Auditors to this extent, and, of course, the Court of Auditors is interested in the fact that, in the context of the municipality of Vienna in particular, I have to add that, as well as by Bank Austria, there is a certain welcome. If I may sum up, I would like to say that, on the one hand, there was a very controversial attitude at the beginning of the Court of Auditors' first examinations by Bank Austria, but that the examination was ultimately brought to a successful conclusion for all parties involved and that a report on the audit could also be submitted to the National Council, which the Court of Auditors considers to be very strong. In addition to the case of examination, which I would like to stress particularly positively, it is the final amendments to the legislation resulting from Bank Austria's refusal to examine, which will enable the Court of Auditors to enforce its audit competence in the future. I see in the support of the National Council for the Court of Auditors a proof of cooperation and I also see in it a manifestation of the link between the National Council and its audit body and a proof that, if the National Council and the Court of Auditors appear together and closed, obstacles can be overcome. This knowledge of cooperation and the joint action between the National Council and the Court of Auditors should actually stimulate both of us.

I cannot understand, ladies and gentlemen of SPÖ, why your top candidate in Lower Austria, Mr Höger, operates with the sensitive question of neutrality of national politics and makes it a topic of election in Lower Austria. He works as a national captain candidate in newspaper inserates. [[Abg. Dipl.-Ing. Kummerer: Because Lower Austria is affected by this!]] I do not know what he has to do with this, Mr President. How can the serious security policy be if your candidate in Lower Austria suddenly acts as a savior of neutrality and warns that the liberals and other forces here in this country want to enter the "evil" NATO and thus endanger Austria? – This is unscrupulous, Mr. Collegue. I would expect from you – since I know that you think differently – that you are also acting against it internally. Otherwise, it leads to uncertainty in the population, and that is irresponsible in the sense of a proper security policy. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] However, I am only waiting, ladies and gentlemen, for the EIA to turn back again, and, as before the EU election, at least in the short term, for a resurgence of neutrality from the speaker's desk. [[Abg. Dr. Maitz: You changed!]] No, we're not turning around, Mr Maitz, but we're for clear conditions. [[Abg. Tichy-Schreiter: Since when?]] We have made it clear that we want to join the alliance – with all our duties, but also with all our rights. It cannot be said, however, that it is only for the people that all those things that are now organized are compatible with neutrality and then conclude all kinds of contracts behind the back of Parliament and the population, in which Austria commits too many things but cannot take advantage of the rights. In my view, this is the wrong way, and we will continue to point it out in the future. It is up to you, and I am already very excited about what the option report will look like, which has been announced so much. It won't be much time for me anymore. Mr Wabl, I would like to inform you in this case only the following: Many pacifists, many politicians, many journalists, who are always against armies and alliances or against the national defense, sometimes come to mind that they have made a mistake. Only they usually come very late on it. One of these pacifists, Wolfgang Brockpähler, wrote in a newspaper article a thanks to the IFOR soldiers in Bosnia – "the thought of a pacifist". I'd like to write you a little bit in the main book. Perhaps it should be borne in mind that it is reasonable to learn how this pacifist gained from the Bosnia-Herzegovina disaster. He says there: "Forgive me, my brother Soldier, that I will be so late to thank you and do your abbite. ... My eyes and those of my Christian sisters and brothers were blinded by righteousness and the fear of letting our beloved prejudices be nourished much too long. ... But now, for the truth's sake, I have to say it openly: You, my brother Soldier – yes, you alone have broken a first line to peace in Bosnia! It was not the peace workers, not the diplomats, not the politicians, not the development workers, not the pastors or theologians. No, it was you – no matter how hard it is to admit it – this time it was really alone!" That is written to you, Mr Wabl, in the main book.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e17691	The next speech is by Mr Moser. Please, honourable Member.
ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e17713	Mr President. High house! I am truly grateful to Mr Wabl for giving us once again the opportunity to express our views on security issues at this first reading today. Mr Wabl! I should like to remind you that we have several requests in the Subcommittee of the Committee on Foreign Policy. I hope that the chairman will, at some point, be convened to this Subcommittee... [[Abg. Arbitrators: Sometime for sure!]] You sure about that? - Wonderful! I also hope that we can really start with the necessary security policy discussion on the prospects of Austrian security policy. I expect the Federal Government to finally present the option report in the near future, so that the House finally comes to a clear statement on what the future of Austrian security and defence policy looks like, ladies and gentlemen. Mr Wabl! I believe that the perspective cannot really lie in it and that we will defend neutrality with teeth and claws, but that it is necessary to take note of the security situation and realities in Europe. It is now true that neutrality as a security policy concept has fulfilled its task. Neutrality was certainly the right answer for our country after the Second World War. It has brought a great deal to our country, and I believe that we should also appreciate it accordingly. However, history has overtaken neutrality and is now about finding a solution to the new challenges we face today and to the challenges of the future. Ladies and gentlemen! From our point of view, the answer can only be solidarity within the framework of the European Community. This also includes meeting and meeting the challenges of security policy together with other countries: be it in the NATO partnership or within the framework of the European Union, that is, within the framework of a common foreign and security policy. We are concerned, ladies and gentlemen, that the European dimension in defence policy is particularly strengthened, that the European Union is also carrying out its tasks in a security-related way, and that we are therefore seeing our priority and the gravity of our activities there. I therefore think that the approach that we hear very often from Mr Maitz and others from the Austrian People's Party – that there is only one way to join NATO or neutrality – is too narrow. It is also useful to draw other perspectives, other possibilities and other options into the calculation. For us, Mr Scheibner, accession to the Western European Union is now also one of these options, the development of European security identity within the framework of the European Union, so that this European Union can fulfil its task on the continent as well as on security issues. We therefore believe that there is the prospect for Austria and that the priority is there. I do not even rule out the fact that Austria can join a transatlantic partnership, as it is the NATO. However, I do not think it would be useful for the national states to be represented within this partnership, but for Austria to be represented within the framework of the European Union. Ladies and gentlemen! As solidarity is required, it will also be necessary to be prepared to make our institutions available to the European Community when it comes to ensuring security on this continent. This is not a good request, Mr Wabl, and therefore we will certainly not give him our consent. - Thank you.

If we organize an exercise within the framework of the "Partnership for Peace", you would allow someone who comes from Sweden, the Czech Republic or Austria to stay in the barracks. However, anyone coming from a NATO army should camp in the tent at the door. Do you think so? I have to ask you this seriously, because you have proposed this legislatively. A second example: They're limited to NATO armies. Does this mean that you would, of course, provide the Ukrainian army or the Swiss army with facilities of the Austrian federal army? ParlaMint-AT_1998-02-26-020-XX-NRSITZ-00110_d7e17836	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! With his request, Mr Wabl puts his finger on the wound of Austrian security policy, namely the question of obsolete and obsolete neutrality from our point of view. I would like to inform you of four statements by important foreign politicians and military officers, who are very familiar with your Minister of Defence and your Foreign Minister, but who, as I believe, have not informed you, and who will also have your chancellor before you from the SPÖ. First of all, I would like to mention the opinion of Dr. Gyarmati, the Hungarian State Secretary at the Ministry of Defence for International Relations: In this opinion, everything relates to the question of what happens when Hungary is a NATO member and the neutral Austria lies in between. Dr. Gyarmati says – I have to translate it quickly – that if something goes wrong and Hungary needs the help of NATO countries, it is assumed that a country that wants to become a NATO member will not make any difficulties if NATO troops and equipment are sent over its territory. What about neutrality? Listen carefully! The Hungarian Chief of Staff said this a little more clearly a few weeks ago. He says that in the SFOR operations so far, all the troops came through Austria, and it is hoped that an Article 5 operation, i.e. in the case of defence, will behave the same way. – That means: It is no longer a talk of Austrian neutrality. [[Abg. Arbitrators: He hopes that!]] Yeah, that's what Hungary is hoping for! Now I'm telling you what the NATO military say: In connection with this question of defence, the U.S. military General Shalikashvili, recently retired, says that it is quite familiar that in the case of military aggression, the whole alliance will rush to the aid of Hungary, carelessly or without regard to whether a non-Member State is being inspected in between. [[Abg. Arbitrators: Yes, NATO is reckless!]] It's literally called "regardless of a non-member country being married in". – That’s the view of NATO! This is the reality: No one will take into account neutrality in a war! The same thing General Wesley Clark said when he was here. He said that Hungary would be supported, so every alliance behaves when it comes to helping its members. We believe that everything else would be unrealistic. Unfortunately, international law has never had much importance in the practice of warfare. The English have also failed to respect international law in the invasion of Norway. They were just a few hours later than the Germans. – This is how the Neutrals do when they are unrealistic! We are of the opinion that: Protection offers only one alliance, so we prefer to be in alliance and talk with – but not against the will of the Austrians by digressing our own people, as is partly tried by the government coalition. Mr Graff is not in vain in today's "Kurier": "Because neutrality has disappeared from the government's language agenda," said former VP Secretary-General Michael Graff to the head of a People's Committee. Its goal: A People's Survey, Neutrality Yes, NATO No'." – That’s how it looks, so you try to disappear over it! However, we liberals are not willing to participate in this game! We are in favour of NATO membership, but we want to inform the Austrians about the real situation, and we will not allow you to disappear over what your government is happy to do. In the SPÖ, too, the breach between the government and the parliamentary group is revealed. Not for nothing have already been rejected by the Committee twice in this context. Who is the head of the government? The chancellor! That is, you sent back your own chancellor's letter with the note "Do not perform homework". In this context, the break in the SPÖ Group is clear, and that means that we do not continue in Austrian security policy. We have offered the ÖVP that if you think security policy in the form you always preach it is so important. But you lack the courage and mum in the bones! Ladies and gentlemen of the ÖVP! They always have great sayings, but nothing goes on. However, this is to a good part – and this must also be mentioned – of your Minister of Defense, who passes by Parliament. He is now back in the process of passing Parliament by concluding agreements with Hungary and various other Eastern European countries and, in turn, making almost military foreign policy, which is not communicated to Parliament. The Foreign Policy Committee has not yet heard of this cooperation agreement, which is to be signed in March, despite the fact that it had already been signed on 18 March. In March, the Minister of Defence of these states in Austria will be crossed for signature. The Committee on Foreign Affairs has not heard of this, and the Committee on Defence has not heard of it yet. Nor is the Minister willing to accept a state of affairs in the Defence Committee. We could see that again today. This is how cooperation with Parliament looks! And then the Minister is surprised when he is thrown in front of his feet and so is not allowed to jump with him. Ladies and gentlemen of the ÖVP! Ask him what this thing looks like! He's not telling you! The diplomats of the Foreign Office know all of this, but they probably didn't tell you either – and probably not the master of the SPÖ! That is what we shall be talking about a few times, because it must be clear. Just as it is now, it can't go on! They provide a bear service to the Austrian security policy if you want to keep the Austrians constantly from hearing the truth and looking at reality. We don't play the liberals, we can assure you!
